  - E -> M -> K -> X -> G -> H -> I

  - E -> M -> L (loop back to M) -> K -> X -> F -> E (loop back to F) -> X -> G -> H -> I

To determine the minimum number of nodes that must be removed to completely disconnect the two black nodes (E and J), we need to identify the paths between these nodes and find the critical nodes whose removal will break all these paths.

### Step-by-Step Analysis:

1. **Identify Direct Connections:**
   - Node E is directly connected to nodes B, I, P, L, and M.
   - Node J is directly connected to nodes P and D.

2. **Identify Indirect Connections:**
   - From E to J through B: E -> B -> C -> N -> O -> Y -> H -> G -> F -> K -> X -> G -> H -> I -> P -> J
   - From E to J through I: E -> I -> P -> J
   - From E to J through P: E -> P -> J
   - From E to J through L: E -> L -> M -> P -> J
   - From E to J through M: E -> M -> P -> J

3. **Identify Critical Nodes:**
   - Removing node P will disconnect E from J through the path E -> P -> J.
   - Removing node I will disconnect E from J through the path E -> I -> P -> J.
